Putting it all together
While Loom is an excellent AI-powered tool for editing screen recordings and generating transcripts, it does not support combining a separate, pre-recorded audio file with a video recording
. The platform offers the ability to trim and stitch together Loom videos, but for complex multilayering of separate video and audio tracks, you will need to use a dedicated video editing application. 
Here is a breakdown of what you can achieve with Loom and which steps require alternative software.
Using Loom's AI features (with limitations)
Loom's built-in tools can handle some aspects of your request, primarily for simplifying and polishing a video that was recorded directly within the application. 

    AI-powered editing: Once you record a screen video and audio simultaneously within Loom, the AI can automatically remove filler words and silences for a more polished result.
    Automatic subtitles: Loom generates automatic captions for your videos by transcribing the audio from your recording. The transcript can be edited for accuracy.
    Basic trimming: You can cut sections from a Loom video using the transcript editor or the audio waveform.
    Stitching Loom videos: You can combine or "stitch" multiple videos that were recorded within Loom into a single video. 

The process for combining separate audio and screen recordings
To accomplish your full request, you must use a more robust video editor.

    Record your assets:
        Screen recording: Use Loom's screen recording feature, but do not record the narration at this time. This gives you a clean screen capture video file.
        Narration audio: Record your audio narration separately using high-quality microphone equipment and an audio recording program like Audacity (free) or Adobe Audition. Recording in a separate session allows you to achieve a higher-quality audio track without the ambient noise from your screen recording.
    Download your Loom video: After recording your screen with Loom, download the video file (e.g., as an MP4) to your computer.
    Use an external video editor: Import both the downloaded screen recording and your separate audio narration file into a video editor. Recommended software includes:
        Adobe Premiere Pro: An industry-standard, professional tool for both Mac and Windows.
        Wondershare DemoCreator: A user-friendly option for recording and editing, which can handle combining separate audio and video tracks.
        Final Cut Pro: A professional editor for macOS with robust multilayering capabilities.
        DaVinci Resolve: A free and highly capable professional editor for all major platforms.
        iMovie: A free, basic video editor for macOS and iOS users.
    Combine and sync your tracks:
        Place your screen recording on one video track in the editor's timeline.
        Place your audio narration on a separate audio track.
        Adjust and align the audio track to precisely sync it with the corresponding visuals in your screen recording.
    Add subtitles:
        Use the editing software to generate a transcript of your audio narration and create a subtitle or caption track.
        Alternatively, you can manually type or copy and paste the transcript into a text layer.
    Add multilayering (if needed):
        If you need to add other visual elements, such as images, text boxes, or other video clips, place these on additional video tracks in the editor's timeline.
        Adjust the timing, size, and position of these layers to fit your needs.
    Export the final video: Once all elements are in place, export the final, combined video file from your editing software.

# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: Loom and OBS Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines the production workflow for creating the final demo video using Loom for screen recording and OBS for enhanced recording capabilities, combined with separate audio narration, subtitles, and multilayering as needed.

## Key Constraint: Loom's Limitations
**Important**: Loom does NOT support combining a separate, pre-recorded audio file with a video recording. While Loom offers excellent AI-powered editing features, complex multilayering requires a dedicated video editing application.

---

## Tools Required

### Recording Tools
- **Loom** (screen recording, basic editing, AI features)
- **OBS Studio** (advanced screen recording alternative)
- **Audacity** (free) or **Adobe Audition** (audio recording)

### Video Editing Software (Choose One)
- **Adobe Premiere Pro** - Industry-standard, professional (Mac/Windows)
- **DaVinci Resolve** - Free, highly capable professional editor (all platforms)
- **Wondershare DemoCreator** - User-friendly, good for tutorials
- **Final Cut Pro** - Professional editor (macOS only)
- **iMovie** - Free, basic editor (macOS/iOS)

---

## Production Workflow

### Phase 1: Asset Recording

#### Step 1.1: Screen Recording (Option A - Loom)
1. Launch Loom
2. Select screen recording mode
3. **DO NOT record narration at this time** - keep microphone muted
4. Record clean screen capture of the demo
5. Review the recording in Loom
6. Download the video file as MP4

**Loom AI Features Available:**
- Auto-remove filler words and silences (if you do record audio)
- Automatic captions/transcripts
- Basic trimming using transcript editor
- Stitching multiple Loom videos together

#### Step 1.2: Screen Recording (Option B - OBS Studio)
1. Launch OBS Studio
2. Configure scenes and sources:
   - Display capture or window capture
   - Set resolution and framerate
3. Disable audio input (no narration yet)
4. Start recording
5. Perform demo
6. Stop and save recording

#### Step 1.3: Audio Narration Recording
1. Set up high-quality microphone equipment
2. Minimize ambient noise in recording environment
3. Open Audacity or Adobe Audition
4. Record audio narration separately:
   - Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
   - Record in segments if needed for quality control
   - Allow for retakes of individual sections
5. Edit audio:
   - Remove unwanted noise
   - Normalize audio levels
   - Remove long pauses
   - Add fade in/fade out
6. Export as high-quality audio file (WAV or MP3 320kbps)

---

### Phase 2: Video Editing and Composition

#### Step 2.1: Import Assets
1. Open your chosen video editing software
2. Create a new project
3. Import the downloaded Loom/OBS screen recording
4. Import the separate audio narration file

#### Step 2.2: Timeline Setup
1. Place screen recording on Video Track 1
2. Place audio narration on Audio Track 1
3. Mute or delete any original audio from the screen recording

#### Step 2.3: Sync Audio to Video
1. Find sync points in both tracks (visual actions matching narration)
2. Align audio to corresponding visuals
3. Make fine adjustments:
   - Trim video sections to match narration pacing
   - Add pauses in video where narration needs more time
   - Speed up slow sections if needed (use sparingly)

#### Step 2.4: Add Subtitles/Captions
**Option A - Auto-generated:**
1. Use video editor's transcription feature
2. Review and correct any errors
3. Adjust timing of subtitle appearance

**Option B - Manual:**
1. Copy transcript from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
2. Create text/subtitle layers
3. Time each subtitle to match audio

**Subtitle Best Practices:**
- Maximum 2 lines per subtitle
- 32-42 characters per line recommended
- Display for 1-7 seconds depending on length
- Ensure readability (size, color, background)

#### Step 2.5: Multilayering (If Needed)
Add additional visual elements on separate tracks:
- **Video Track 2**: Overlay graphics, logos, watermarks
- **Video Track 3**: Text callouts, annotations, highlights
- **Video Track 4**: Picture-in-picture webcam footage
- **Additional Audio Tracks**: Background music, sound effects

**Elements to Consider:**
- Title cards (intro/outro)
- Section transitions
- Highlighted UI elements
- Zoomed sections for detail
- Animated arrows or circles to draw attention
- Lower thirds for presenter information
- Background music (keep volume low, ~10-15%)

---

### Phase 3: Polish and Enhancement

#### Step 3.1: Apply Transitions
- Add subtle transitions between major sections
- Avoid overusing flashy effects
- Maintain professional appearance

#### Step 3.2: Color Correction (Optional)
- Adjust brightness/contrast for consistency
- Ensure screen content is clearly visible
- Match color grade across all clips

#### Step 3.3: Audio Mixing
- Balance narration volume
- Add background music if desired (low volume)
- Ensure no audio clipping or distortion
- Apply audio compression for consistent levels

#### Step 3.4: Final Review
- Watch entire video start to finish
- Check for:
  - Audio sync issues
  - Subtitle timing and accuracy
  - Visual clarity
  - Smooth transitions
  - Appropriate pacing
  - Any jarring cuts or errors

---

### Phase 4: Export and Delivery

#### Step 4.1: Export Settings
**Recommended Settings:**
- Format: MP4 (H.264)
- Resolution: 1920x1080 (1080p) or 3840x2160 (4K)
- Frame Rate: Match source (typically 30fps or 60fps)
- Bitrate: 8-12 Mbps for 1080p, 35-45 Mbps for 4K
- Audio: AAC, 320 kbps, 48kHz

#### Step 4.2: Quality Check
1. Watch exported video completely
2. Verify file size is reasonable for sharing
3. Test on different devices if possible
4. Confirm audio/video quality maintained

#### Step 4.3: Backup and Archive
- Save project files
- Keep original assets (screen recording, audio, etc.)
- Store multiple copies of final export

---

## Quick Reference: Loom's Built-in Capabilities

### What Loom CAN Do:
✅ Record screen with simultaneous audio narration  
✅ AI-powered removal of filler words and silences  
✅ Automatic caption/subtitle generation  
✅ Basic trimming using transcript or waveform  
✅ Stitching multiple Loom videos together  
✅ Share and collaborate easily  

### What Loom CANNOT Do:
❌ Import and combine separate audio files  
❌ Advanced multilayer editing  
❌ Complex transitions and effects  
❌ Picture-in-picture with external sources  
❌ Advanced color grading  
❌ Multiple track audio mixing  

**Therefore**: Use Loom for quick recording and basic edits, but use professional video editing software for the final production with separate audio tracks and multilayering.

---

## Alternative Workflow: All-in-Loom (Simplified)

If you want to minimize external editing:

1. **Record everything in Loom simultaneously**:
   - Screen capture + live narration
   - No separate audio track needed

2. **Use Loom's AI editing**:
   - Auto-remove filler words
   - Trim using transcript editor
   - Generate automatic captions

3. **Limitations**:
   - Cannot separate audio/video tracks
   - Less control over audio quality
   - Limited multilayering options
   - Cannot add background music or complex graphics

**Recommended For**: Quick demos, internal presentations, casual content  
**Not Recommended For**: Professional submissions, marketing content, complex tutorials

---

## Timeline Estimate

| Phase | Task | Estimated Time |
|-------|------|----------------|
| **Prep** | Script review, equipment setup | 30 min |
| **Recording** | Screen recording (OBS/Loom) | 45-60 min |
| **Recording** | Audio narration | 60-90 min |
| **Editing** | Import, sync, basic cuts | 2-3 hours |
| **Editing** | Subtitles, multilayering | 2-3 hours |
| **Polish** | Transitions, effects, review | 1-2 hours |
| **Export** | Render and quality check | 30-60 min |
| **TOTAL** | | **8-12 hours** |

*Times are estimates and will vary based on experience and complexity.*

---

## Next Steps

1. ✅ Review FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md and FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
2. ⬜ Set up recording environment (lighting, audio, screen)
3. ⬜ Test recording equipment (both video and audio)
4. ⬜ Record screen capture (Loom or OBS)
5. ⬜ Record audio narration separately
6. ⬜ Import assets into video editor
7. ⬜ Edit, sync, and enhance
8. ⬜ Add subtitles and multilayering
9. ⬜ Export final video
10. ⬜ Review and submit

---

## Resources

- **Loom Help Center**: https://support.loom.com/
- **OBS Studio Documentation**: https://obsproject.com/wiki/
- **Audacity Manual**: https://manual.audacityteam.org/
- **DaVinci Resolve Training**: https://www.blackmagicdesign.com/products/davinciresolve/training

---

## Notes

- Prioritize audio quality - viewers will forgive mediocre video but not bad audio
- Practice your narration before recording to minimize retakes
- Keep backup copies of all assets throughout the process
- Consider doing a test run with a short segment before full production
- If using background music, ensure you have proper licensing

---

# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + Blender Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **Blender** (audio stripping and replacement)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **Blender** (Free, open-source video editor)
   - ⬜ Download from https://www.blender.org/download/
   - ⬜ Install on Windows
   - ⬜ Learn basic Video Editing workspace (tutorial links below)
   - **Note**: Blender is primarily 3D software but has powerful built-in video editor

### 🎯 Alternative: Free AI-Enabled Video Editing Websites
If you prefer not to install Blender, consider these online alternatives:

**Top Recommendations:**
1. **Clipchamp** (Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - Web-based, no download needed
   - AI auto-captions
   - Easy audio replacement
   - Direct integration with OneDrive
   - https://clipchamp.com/

2. **CapCut Online** (Free)
   - AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - Simple audio track replacement
   - Export up to 1080p free
   - https://www.capcut.com/

3. **VEED.io** (Free tier available)
   - AI subtitles and transcription
   - Audio replacement
   - Clean interface
   - https://www.veed.io/

4. **Kapwing** (Free tier)
   - AI-powered editing tools
   - Collaborative features
   - Audio track swapping
   - https://www.kapwing.com/

5. **Canva Video Editor** (Free tier)
   - User-friendly interface
   - Limited AI features
   - Good for basic edits
   - https://www.canva.com/

**Comparison:**
| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **Clipchamp** | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | Quick edits, Windows users |
| **CapCut** | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | Social media, trendy effects |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → Blender/Online Editor

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio (Choose Your Tool)

## Option A: Using Blender (Free, Most Powerful)

### Setup Blender First Time
1. Download from https://www.blender.org/download/
2. Install (takes 5-10 minutes)
3. Launch Blender
4. Switch to **Video Editing** workspace:
   - Click "Video Editing" at the top of the window
   - OR: File → New → Video Editing

### Blender Workflow
1. **Import Video**:
   - Drag `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4` into timeline
   - It will appear as video and audio strips

2. **Remove Original Audio**:
   - Select the audio strip (green waveform)
   - Press Delete or X → Delete
   - Now you have silent video

3. **Import New Narration**:
   - Add → Sound → `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
   - Audio strip appears in timeline

4. **Sync Audio to Video**:
   - Drag audio strip to align with video
   - Use G key to grab/move selected strips
   - Zoom in/out with mouse wheel for precision
   - Play preview to check sync (Spacebar)

5. **Trim and Adjust**:
   - Select strips and press K to cut/knife tool
   - Drag strip edges to trim
   - Make sure video and audio end at same time

6. **Add Transitions (Optional)**:
   - Shift+A → Effect Strip → Cross
   - Drag over cuts between clips

7. **Export Final Video**:
   - Go to Output Properties (printer icon on right)
   - File Format: **FFmpeg video**
   - Container: **MPEG-4**
   - Video Codec: **H.264**
   - Audio Codec: **AAC**
   - Quality: **Perceptually Lossless** or **High Quality**
   - Resolution: 1920x1080
   - Frame Rate: Match source (30 or 60 fps)
   - Output: Choose save location `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`
   - Click **Render** → **Render Animation**

**Blender Video Editing Resources:**
- Official Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Quick Start Guide: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/index.html

---

## Option B: Using Online Editor (Easier, Faster)

### Recommended: CapCut Online (Best Free Features)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

### Alternative: Clipchamp (Good for Windows Users)

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles
If your chosen tool supports auto-captions:
1. Generate auto-captions/subtitles
2. Review for accuracy (especially technical terms)
3. Edit any mistakes
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (white text, black background/shadow)

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds)
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking

| **Editing** | Combine video + audio in Blender/Online | 30-60 min |
| **Editing** | Add subtitles/captions | 30-45 min |
| **Polish** | Final review and tweaks | 15-30 min |
| **Export** | Render final video | 10-20 min |
| **TOTAL** | | **4-6 hours** |

*Significantly faster than full production workflow!*

---

## Decision Helper: Which Video Editor Should You Use?

### Choose **Blender** if:
✅ You want completely free, no limitations, no watermarks  
✅ You're comfortable with a learning curve  
✅ You want offline editing  
✅ You might need advanced features later  
✅ File privacy is important (all local)  

### Choose **CapCut Online** if:
✅ You want the easiest, fastest option  
✅ You want AI auto-captions  
✅ You don't mind web-based editing  
✅ You want trendy effects/transitions  
✅ You're okay with account creation  

### Choose **Clipchamp** if:
✅ You're on Windows 11 (comes pre-installed)  
✅ You have Microsoft account already  
✅ You want simple, clean interface  
✅ You trust Microsoft with your files  

### Choose **DaVinci Resolve** if:
✅ You want professional-grade free software  
✅ You're willing to invest time learning  
✅ You need color grading capabilities  
✅ You have powerful computer (GPU required)  
⚠️ **Warning**: Steeper learning curve than Blender for simple tasks  

**My Recommendation for You**: Start with **CapCut Online** for speed and ease. You can always use Blender later if you need more control or for future videos.

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "Blender is too confusing"
- Switch to CapCut or Clipchamp (much simpler)
- Watch beginner tutorial first
- Start with just audio replacement, skip effects
- Ask for help in Blender forums

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

```markdown
# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (primary video editor - audio stripping and replacement)
- **Clipchamp** (complementary/backup editor if needed for additional features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **Blender** (Free, open-source video editor)
   - ⬜ Download from https://www.blender.org/download/
   - ⬜ Install on Windows
   - ⬜ Learn basic Video Editing workspace (tutorial links below)
   - **Note**: Blender is primarily 3D software but has powerful built-in video editor

### 🎯 Alternative: Free AI-Enabled Video Editing Websites
If you prefer not to install Blender, consider these online alternatives:

**Top Recommendations:**
1. **Clipchamp** (Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - Web-based, no download needed
   - AI auto-captions
   - Easy audio replacement
   - Direct integration with OneDrive
   - https://clipchamp.com/

2. **CapCut Online** (Free)
   - AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - Simple audio track replacement
   - Export up to 1080p free
   - https://www.capcut.com/

3. **VEED.io** (Free tier available)
   - AI subtitles and transcription
   - Audio replacement
   - Clean interface
   - https://www.veed.io/

4. **Kapwing** (Free tier)
   - AI-powered editing tools
   - Collaborative features
   - Audio track swapping
   - https://www.kapwing.com/

5. **Canva Video Editor** (Free tier)
   - User-friendly interface
   - Limited AI features
   - Good for basic edits
   - https://www.canva.com/

**Comparison:**
| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **Clipchamp** | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | Quick edits, Windows users |
| **CapCut** | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | Social media, trendy effects |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → Blender/Online Editor

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio (Choose Your Tool)

## Option A: Using Blender (Free, Most Powerful)

### Setup Blender First Time
1. Download from https://www.blender.org/download/
2. Install (takes 5-10 minutes)
3. Launch Blender
4. Switch to **Video Editing** workspace:
   - Click "Video Editing" at the top of the window
   - OR: File → New → Video Editing

### Blender Workflow
1. **Import Video**:
   - Drag `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4` into timeline
   - It will appear as video and audio strips

2. **Remove Original Audio**:
   - Select the audio strip (green waveform)
   - Press Delete or X → Delete
   - Now you have silent video

3. **Import New Narration**:
   - Add → Sound → `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
   - Audio strip appears in timeline

4. **Sync Audio to Video**:
   - Drag audio strip to align with video
   - Use G key to grab/move selected strips
   - Zoom in/out with mouse wheel for precision
   - Play preview to check sync (Spacebar)

5. **Trim and Adjust**:
   - Select strips and press K to cut/knife tool
   - Drag strip edges to trim
   - Make sure video and audio end at same time

6. **Add Transitions (Optional)**:
   - Shift+A → Effect Strip → Cross
   - Drag over cuts between clips

7. **Export Final Video**:
   - Go to Output Properties (printer icon on right)
   - File Format: **FFmpeg video**
   - Container: **MPEG-4**
   - Video Codec: **H.264**
   - Audio Codec: **AAC**
   - Quality: **Perceptually Lossless** or **High Quality**
   - Resolution: 1920x1080
   - Frame Rate: Match source (30 or 60 fps)
   - Output: Choose save location `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`
   - Click **Render** → **Render Animation**

**Blender Video Editing Resources:**
- Official Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Quick Start Guide: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/index.html

---

## Option B: Using Online Editor (Easier, Faster)

### Recommended: CapCut Online (Best Free Features)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

### Alternative: Clipchamp (Good for Windows Users)

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles
If your chosen tool supports auto-captions:
1. Generate auto-captions/subtitles
2. Review for accuracy (especially technical terms)
3. Edit any mistakes
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (white text, black background/shadow)

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds)
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking

# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (primary video editor - audio stripping and replacement)
- **Clipchamp** (complementary/backup editor if needed for additional features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (PRIMARY video editor - Free, AI-powered)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - **Your Choice**: Easiest and fastest option

3. **Clipchamp** (COMPLEMENTARY video editor - Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - ⬜ Optional: Visit https://clipchamp.com/ or use from Windows 11 Start menu
   - ⬜ Create/sign in with Microsoft account
   - ⬜ Use if CapCut lacks specific features you need
   - ⬜ Good for additional effects or polish

### 🎯 Alternative: Free AI-Enabled Video Editing Websites
If you prefer not to install Blender, consider these online alternatives:

**Top Recommendations:**
1. **Clipchamp** (Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - Web-based, no download needed
   - AI auto-captions
   - Easy audio replacement
   - Direct integration with OneDrive
   - https://clipchamp.com/

2. **CapCut Online** (Free)
   - AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - Simple audio track replacement
   - Export up to 1080p free
   - https://www.capcut.com/

3. **VEED.io** (Free tier available)
   - AI subtitles and transcription
   - Audio replacement
   - Clean interface
   - https://www.veed.io/

4. **Kapwing** (Free tier)
   - AI-powered editing tools
   - Collaborative features
   - Audio track swapping
   - https://www.kapwing.com/

5. **Canva Video Editor** (Free tier)
   - User-friendly interface
   - Limited AI features
   - Good for basic edits
   - https://www.canva.com/

**Comparison:**
| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **Clipchamp** | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | Quick edits, Windows users |
| **CapCut** | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | Social media, trendy effects |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → Blender/Online Editor

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio (Choose Your Tool)

## Option A: Using Blender (Free, Most Powerful)

### Setup Blender First Time
1. Download from https://www.blender.org/download/
2. Install (takes 5-10 minutes)
3. Launch Blender
4. Switch to **Video Editing** workspace:
   - Click "Video Editing" at the top of the window
   - OR: File → New → Video Editing

### Blender Workflow
1. **Import Video**:
   - Drag `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4` into timeline
   - It will appear as video and audio strips

2. **Remove Original Audio**:
   - Select the audio strip (green waveform)
   - Press Delete or X → Delete
   - Now you have silent video

3. **Import New Narration**:
   - Add → Sound → `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
   - Audio strip appears in timeline

4. **Sync Audio to Video**:
   - Drag audio strip to align with video
   - Use G key to grab/move selected strips
   - Zoom in/out with mouse wheel for precision
   - Play preview to check sync (Spacebar)

5. **Trim and Adjust**:
   - Select strips and press K to cut/knife tool
   - Drag strip edges to trim
   - Make sure video and audio end at same time

6. **Add Transitions (Optional)**:
   - Shift+A → Effect Strip → Cross
   - Drag over cuts between clips

7. **Export Final Video**:
   - Go to Output Properties (printer icon on right)
   - File Format: **FFmpeg video**
   - Container: **MPEG-4**
   - Video Codec: **H.264**
   - Audio Codec: **AAC**
   - Quality: **Perceptually Lossless** or **High Quality**
   - Resolution: 1920x1080
   - Frame Rate: Match source (30 or 60 fps)
   - Output: Choose save location `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`
   - Click **Render** → **Render Animation**

**Blender Video Editing Resources:**
- Official Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Quick Start Guide: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/index.html

---

## Option B: Using Online Editor (Easier, Faster)

### Recommended: CapCut Online (Best Free Features)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

### Alternative: Clipchamp (Good for Windows Users)

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles
If your chosen tool supports auto-captions:
1. Generate auto-captions/subtitles
2. Review for accuracy (especially technical terms)
3. Edit any mistakes
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (white text, black background/shadow)

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds)
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking

---

## Complete Workflow Summary

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│ • Set up Loom account                                       │
│ • Record screen + audio simultaneously                      │

│ • Download MP4                                              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                            ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│ • Record high-quality audio narration                       │
│ • Edit: noise reduction, normalize, trim                    │
│ • Export as WAV or MP3                                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                            ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PHASE 3: COMBINE IN BLENDER OR ONLINE EDITOR               │
│                                                              │
│ Option A: BLENDER                │ Option B: CAPCUT/CLIPCHAMP│
│ • Import video                   │ • Upload both files       │
│ • Delete original audio          │ • Detach original audio   │
│ • Add new narration              │ • Delete original audio   │
│ • Sync                           │ • Add new narration       │
│ • Export MP4                     │ • Sync                    │
│                                  │ • Export MP4              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                            ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PHASE 4: FINAL POLISH (OPTIONAL)                            │
│ • Add auto-captions/subtitles                               │
│ • Add title cards                                           │
│ • Review and quality check                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

## Timeline Estimate (Your Workflow)

| Phase | Task | Estimated Time |
|-------|------|----------------|
| **Setup** | Create Loom account | 5 min |
| **Setup** | Install Blender (if using) | 10 min |
| **Setup** | Learn basic Blender video editing | 30-45 min |
| **Recording** | Loom screen + audio recording | 30-45 min |
| **Recording** | Audacity narration recording | 45-60 min |
| **Recording** | Audio editing in Audacity | 30-45 min |
| **Editing** | Combine video + audio in Blender/Online | 30-60 min |
| **Editing** | Add subtitles/captions | 30-45 min |
| **Polish** | Final review and tweaks | 15-30 min |
| **Export** | Render final video | 10-20 min |
| **TOTAL** | | **4-6 hours** |

*Significantly faster than full production workflow!*

---

## Decision Helper: Which Video Editor Should You Use?

### Choose **Blender** if:
✅ You want completely free, no limitations, no watermarks  
✅ You're comfortable with a learning curve  
✅ You want offline editing  
✅ You might need advanced features later  
✅ File privacy is important (all local)  

### Choose **CapCut Online** if:
✅ You want the easiest, fastest option  
✅ You want AI auto-captions  
✅ You don't mind web-based editing  
✅ You want trendy effects/transitions  
✅ You're okay with account creation  

### Choose **Clipchamp** if:
✅ You're on Windows 11 (comes pre-installed)  
✅ You have Microsoft account already  
✅ You want simple, clean interface  
✅ You trust Microsoft with your files  

### Choose **DaVinci Resolve** if:
✅ You want professional-grade free software  
✅ You're willing to invest time learning  
✅ You need color grading capabilities  
✅ You have powerful computer (GPU required)  
⚠️ **Warning**: Steeper learning curve than Blender for simple tasks  

**My Recommendation for You**: Start with **CapCut Online** for speed and ease. You can always use Blender later if you need more control or for future videos.

---

---

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "Blender is too confusing"
- Switch to CapCut or Clipchamp (much simpler)
- Watch beginner tutorial first
- Start with just audio replacement, skip effects
- Ask for help in Blender forums

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

---

## Resources

### Loom
- Sign up: https://www.loom.com/signup
- Help Center: https://support.loom.com/
- Video tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bf28AeztF9c

### Audacity
- Download: https://www.audacityteam.org/download/
- Manual: https://manual.audacityteam.org/
- Noise Reduction Guide: https://manual.audacityteam.org/man/noise_reduction.html

### Blender Video Editing
- Download: https://www.blender.org/download/
- Video Editing Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Manual: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/

### Online Editors
- CapCut: https://www.capcut.com/
- Clipchamp: https://clipchamp.com/
- VEED.io: https://www.veed.io/
- Kapwing: https://www.kapwing.com/

### Royalty-Free Music
- YouTube Audio Library: https://studio.youtube.com/
- Incompetech: https://incompetech.com/music/
- Free Music Archive: https://freemusicarchive.org/

---

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

---

*Document created: October 19, 2025*  
*For: CollabCanvas Demo Video - Final Submission*  
*Updated workflow based on user preferences*

   - Make sure video and audio end at same time

6. **Add Transitions (Optional)**:
   - Shift+A → Effect Strip → Cross
   - Drag over cuts between clips

7. **Export Final Video**:
   - Go to Output Properties (printer icon on right)
   - File Format: **FFmpeg video**
   - Container: **MPEG-4**
   - Video Codec: **H.264**
   - Audio Codec: **AAC**
   - Quality: **Perceptually Lossless** or **High Quality**
   - Resolution: 1920x1080
   - Frame Rate: Match source (30 or 60 fps)
   - Output: Choose save location `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`
   - Click **Render** → **Render Animation**

**Blender Video Editing Resources:**
- Official Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Quick Start Guide: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/index.html

---

## Option B: Using Online Editor (Easier, Faster)

### Recommended: CapCut Online (Best Free Features)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

### Alternative: Clipchamp (Good for Windows Users)

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles
If your chosen tool supports auto-captions:
1. Generate auto-captions/subtitles
2. Review for accuracy (especially technical terms)
3. Edit any mistakes
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (white text, black background/shadow)

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds)
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking

## Decision Helper: Which Video Editor Should You Use?

### Choose **Blender** if:
✅ You want completely free, no limitations, no watermarks  
✅ You're comfortable with a learning curve  
✅ You want offline editing  
✅ You might need advanced features later  
✅ File privacy is important (all local)  

### Choose **CapCut Online** if:
✅ You want the easiest, fastest option  
✅ You want AI auto-captions  
✅ You don't mind web-based editing  
✅ You want trendy effects/transitions  
✅ You're okay with account creation  

### Choose **Clipchamp** if:
✅ You're on Windows 11 (comes pre-installed)  
✅ You have Microsoft account already  
✅ You want simple, clean interface  
✅ You trust Microsoft with your files  

### Choose **DaVinci Resolve** if:
✅ You want professional-grade free software  
✅ You're willing to invest time learning  
✅ You need color grading capabilities  
✅ You have powerful computer (GPU required)  
⚠️ **Warning**: Steeper learning curve than Blender for simple tasks  

**My Recommendation for You**: Start with **CapCut Online** for speed and ease. You can always use Blender later if you need more control or for future videos.

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "Blender is too confusing"
- Switch to CapCut or Clipchamp (much simpler)
- Watch beginner tutorial first
- Start with just audio replacement, skip effects
- Ask for help in Blender forums

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

---

# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (primary video editor - audio stripping and replacement)
- **Clipchamp** (complementary/backup editor if needed for additional features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (PRIMARY video editor - Free, AI-powered)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - **Your Choice**: Easiest and fastest option

3. **Clipchamp** (COMPLEMENTARY video editor - Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - ⬜ Optional: Visit https://clipchamp.com/ or use from Windows 11 Start menu
   - ⬜ Create/sign in with Microsoft account
   - ⬜ Use if CapCut lacks specific features you need
   - ⬜ Good for additional effects or polish

### 🎯 Your Editor Combination Strategy

**Primary:** CapCut Online + **Backup/Complement:** Clipchamp

This combination gives you:
- ✅ **CapCut's AI features**: Auto-captions, voice enhancement, trendy effects
- ✅ **Clipchamp's reliability**: Microsoft integration, Windows-friendly, stable platform
- ✅ **Flexibility**: If one tool lacks a feature, switch to the other
- ✅ **No installation needed**: Both are browser-based
- ✅ **Free 1080p export**: Both support high-quality output

**When to Use Each:**
- **CapCut**: Primary editing, AI captions, audio replacement, effects
- **Clipchamp**: Backup if CapCut has issues, additional polish, OneDrive integration

### 🔧 Alternative Tools (Reference Only)
If you need them later, here are other options:

| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **CapCut** ⭐ | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | **Your primary choice** |
| **Clipchamp** ⭐ | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | **Your backup/complement** |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio (Choose Your Tool)

## Option A: Using Blender (Free, Most Powerful)

### Setup Blender First Time
1. Download from https://www.blender.org/download/
2. Install (takes 5-10 minutes)
3. Launch Blender
4. Switch to **Video Editing** workspace:
   - Click "Video Editing" at the top of the window
   - OR: File → New → Video Editing

### Blender Workflow
1. **Import Video**:
   - Drag `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4` into timeline
   - It will appear as video and audio strips

2. **Remove Original Audio**:
   - Select the audio strip (green waveform)
   - Press Delete or X → Delete
   - Now you have silent video

3. **Import New Narration**:
   - Add → Sound → `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
   - Audio strip appears in timeline

4. **Sync Audio to Video**:
   - Drag audio strip to align with video
   - Use G key to grab/move selected strips
   - Zoom in/out with mouse wheel for precision
   - Play preview to check sync (Spacebar)

5. **Trim and Adjust**:
   - Select strips and press K to cut/knife tool
   - Drag strip edges to trim
   - Make sure video and audio end at same time

6. **Add Transitions (Optional)**:
   - Shift+A → Effect Strip → Cross
   - Drag over cuts between clips

7. **Export Final Video**:
   - Go to Output Properties (printer icon on right)
   - File Format: **FFmpeg video**
   - Container: **MPEG-4**
   - Video Codec: **H.264**
   - Audio Codec: **AAC**
   - Quality: **Perceptually Lossless** or **High Quality**
   - Resolution: 1920x1080
   - Frame Rate: Match source (30 or 60 fps)
   - Output: Choose save location `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`
   - Click **Render** → **Render Animation**

**Blender Video Editing Resources:**
- Official Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Quick Start Guide: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/index.html
