## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "Blender is too confusing"
- Switch to CapCut or Clipchamp (much simpler)
- Watch beginner tutorial first
- Start with just audio replacement, skip effects
- Ask for help in Blender forums

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder

- ✅ Easiest, fastest option - minimal learning curve
- ✅ AI auto-captions built-in - saves tons of time
- ✅ Web-based - no installation, works anywhere
- ✅ Trendy effects/transitions - professional look
- ✅ Free 1080p export - perfect quality for your demo
- ✅ Intuitive interface - similar to mobile video apps

### ✅ Clipchamp (Backup/Complement)
**Great complementary choice:**
- ✅ Windows-friendly - may already be installed on Windows 11
- ✅ Microsoft integration - use existing account
- ✅ Reliable platform - stable, well-supported
- ✅ Additional features if needed - backup plan
- ✅ OneDrive integration - easy file management

### 📊 Why This Combo Works
- **Speed**: Get your video done in 3.5-5 hours instead of 8-12
- **Simplicity**: Both have low learning curves
- **Flexibility**: If one lacks a feature, try the other
- **Quality**: Both export professional 1080p video
- **AI Features**: Auto-captions save manual work

### 🎯 Other Tools (For Reference Only)
You can explore these later if needed:
- **Blender**: If you want offline editing with no limits
- **DaVinci Resolve**: If you need professional color grading
- **VEED.io / Kapwing**: Alternative online editors

---

## Next Steps Checklist

### Setup Phase
- [ ] Create Loom account at https://www.loom.com/signup
- [ ] Test Loom with 30-second recording
- [ ] Ensure Audacity is installed and working
- [ ] Test microphone levels in Audacity
- [ ] ✅ **Decision Made**: Using CapCut Online + Clipchamp backup
- [ ] Create CapCut account at https://www.capcut.com/
- [ ] Test CapCut interface with sample upload
- [ ] Optional: Set up Clipchamp at https://clipchamp.com/

### Recording Phase
- [ ] Review FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
- [ ] Set up quiet recording environment
- [ ] Record screen demo in Loom (with audio)
- [ ] Download Loom MP4
- [ ] Watch Loom video while recording narration in Audacity
- [ ] Edit narration audio
- [ ] Export narration as WAV

### Editing Phase (CapCut Online)
- [ ] Upload video and audio files to CapCut
- [ ] Drag video to timeline
- [ ] Detach and delete original audio from video
- [ ] Add new narration audio to timeline
- [ ] Sync audio to video (drag to align)
- [ ] Generate AI auto-captions
- [ ] Review and edit captions for accuracy
- [ ] Add title cards (optional)
- [ ] Add transitions between sections (optional)
- [ ] Preview entire video multiple times

### Export Phase
- [ ] Export final video from CapCut (1080p MP4)
- [ ] Download exported video to project folder
- [ ] Rename: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`
- [ ] Watch complete exported video start-to-finish
- [ ] Check audio/video sync throughout
- [ ] Verify subtitle timing and accuracy
- [ ] Test playback on different device
- [ ] Back up all source files (Loom recording, Audacity audio, final video)

### Submission Phase
- [ ] Upload to required platform
- [ ] Verify upload successful
- [ ] Submit link/file as required
- [ ] Celebrate! 🎉

---

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "Blender is too confusing"
- Switch to CapCut or Clipchamp (much simpler)
- Watch beginner tutorial first
- Start with just audio replacement, skip effects
- Ask for help in Blender forums

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

---

## Resources

### Loom
- Sign up: https://www.loom.com/signup
- Help Center: https://support.loom.com/
- Video tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Bf28AeztF9c

### Audacity
- Download: https://www.audacityteam.org/download/
- Manual: https://manual.audacityteam.org/
- Noise Reduction Guide: https://manual.audacityteam.org/man/noise_reduction.html

### Blender Video Editing
- Download: https://www.blender.org/download/
- Video Editing Tutorial: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mGY-6FGv5mY
- Manual: https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/video_editing/

### Online Editors
- CapCut: https://www.capcut.com/
- Clipchamp: https://clipchamp.com/
- VEED.io: https://www.veed.io/
- Kapwing: https://www.kapwing.com/

### Royalty-Free Music
- YouTube Audio Library: https://studio.youtube.com/
- Incompetech: https://incompetech.com/music/
- Free Music Archive: https://freemusicarchive.org/

---

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

```markdown
# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (primary video editor - audio stripping and replacement)
- **Clipchamp** (complementary/backup editor if needed for additional features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (PRIMARY video editor - Free, AI-powered)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - **Your Choice**: Easiest and fastest option

3. **Clipchamp** (COMPLEMENTARY video editor - Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - ⬜ Optional: Visit https://clipchamp.com/ or use from Windows 11 Start menu
   - ⬜ Create/sign in with Microsoft account
   - ⬜ Use if CapCut lacks specific features you need
   - ⬜ Good for additional effects or polish

### 🎯 Your Editor Combination Strategy

**Primary:** CapCut Online + **Backup/Complement:** Clipchamp

This combination gives you:
- ✅ **CapCut's AI features**: Auto-captions, voice enhancement, trendy effects
- ✅ **Clipchamp's reliability**: Microsoft integration, Windows-friendly, stable platform
- ✅ **Flexibility**: If one tool lacks a feature, switch to the other
- ✅ **No installation needed**: Both are browser-based
- ✅ **Free 1080p export**: Both support high-quality output

**When to Use Each:**
- **CapCut**: Primary editing, AI captions, audio replacement, effects
- **Clipchamp**: Backup if CapCut has issues, additional polish, OneDrive integration

### 🔧 Alternative Tools (Reference Only)
If you need them later, here are other options:

| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **CapCut** ⭐ | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | **Your primary choice** |
| **Clipchamp** ⭐ | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | **Your backup/complement** |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

## Your Primary Tool: CapCut Online (Easiest, Fastest)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

---

   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)

- **Clipchamp**: Backup if CapCut has issues, additional polish, OneDrive integration

### 🔧 Alternative Tools (Reference Only)
If you need them later, here are other options:

| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **CapCut** ⭐ | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | **Your primary choice** |
| **Clipchamp** ⭐ | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | **Your backup/complement** |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

## Your Primary Tool: CapCut Online (Easiest, Fastest)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "CapCut isn't working or is missing a feature"
- Switch to Clipchamp as backup
- Both tools have similar interfaces
- Check if browser needs updating
- Try incognito/private mode
- Clear browser cache and cookies

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "CapCut isn't working or is missing a feature"
- Switch to Clipchamp as backup
- Both tools have similar interfaces
- Check if browser needs updating
- Try incognito/private mode
- Clear browser cache and cookies

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:

   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (PRIMARY video editor - Free, AI-powered)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - **Your Choice**: Easiest and fastest option

3. **Clipchamp** (COMPLEMENTARY video editor - Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - ⬜ Optional: Visit https://clipchamp.com/ or use from Windows 11 Start menu
   - ⬜ Create/sign in with Microsoft account
   - ⬜ Use if CapCut lacks specific features you need
   - ⬜ Good for additional effects or polish

### 🎯 Your Editor Combination Strategy

**Primary:** CapCut Online + **Backup/Complement:** Clipchamp

This combination gives you:
- ✅ **CapCut's AI features**: Auto-captions, voice enhancement, trendy effects
- ✅ **Clipchamp's reliability**: Microsoft integration, Windows-friendly, stable platform
- ✅ **Flexibility**: If one tool lacks a feature, switch to the other
- ✅ **No installation needed**: Both are browser-based
- ✅ **Free 1080p export**: Both support high-quality output

**When to Use Each:**

- **Clipchamp**: Backup if CapCut has issues, additional polish, OneDrive integration

### 🔧 Alternative Tools (Reference Only)

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

## Your Primary Tool: CapCut Online (Easiest, Fastest)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

- Incompetech: https://incompetech.com/music/
- Free Music Archive: https://freemusicarchive.org/

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

---

---

**Your Tools:**
1. ✅ Loom - Record screen demo with audio
2. ✅ Audacity - Record high-quality narration
3. ✅ CapCut Online - Primary video editor
4. ✅ Clipchamp - Backup/complement if needed

**Your Workflow:**
1. Record in Loom → Download MP4
2. Record narration in Audacity → Export WAV
3. Upload both to CapCut → Replace audio → Add AI captions
4. Export final video → Done!

**Estimated Time:** 3.5-5 hours total

**Why This Works:**
- Fastest path to professional results
- No software installation required (all online)
- AI features save time on captions
- Backup option ready if needed

# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: UPDATED - Loom + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (primary video editor - audio stripping and replacement)
- **Clipchamp** (complementary/backup editor if needed for additional features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (PRIMARY video editor - Free, AI-powered)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - **Your Choice**: Easiest and fastest option

3. **Clipchamp** (COMPLEMENTARY video editor - Free, Microsoft-owned)
   - ⬜ Optional: Visit https://clipchamp.com/ or use from Windows 11 Start menu
   - ⬜ Create/sign in with Microsoft account
   - ⬜ Use if CapCut lacks specific features you need
   - ⬜ Good for additional effects or polish

### 🎯 Your Editor Combination Strategy

**Primary:** CapCut Online + **Backup/Complement:** Clipchamp

This combination gives you:
- ✅ **CapCut's AI features**: Auto-captions, voice enhancement, trendy effects
- ✅ **Clipchamp's reliability**: Microsoft integration, Windows-friendly, stable platform
- ✅ **Flexibility**: If one tool lacks a feature, switch to the other
- ✅ **No installation needed**: Both are browser-based
- ✅ **Free 1080p export**: Both support high-quality output

**When to Use Each:**
- **CapCut**: Primary editing, AI captions, audio replacement, effects
- **Clipchamp**: Backup if CapCut has issues, additional polish, OneDrive integration

### 🔧 Alternative Tools (Reference Only)
If you need them later, here are other options:

| Tool | AI Features | Export Quality (Free) | Learning Curve | Best For |
|------|-------------|----------------------|----------------|----------|
| **CapCut** ⭐ | Many AI tools | 1080p | Low | **Your primary choice** |
| **Clipchamp** ⭐ | Auto-captions | 1080p | Low | **Your backup/complement** |
| **VEED.io** | Auto-captions | 720p | Low | Online workflow, subtitles |
| **Kapwing** | Some AI | 1080p (watermark) | Low | Collaboration |
| **Blender** | No | Unlimited | High | Full control, offline |
| **DaVinci Resolve** | No | Unlimited | Very High | Professional quality |

---

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

## Your Primary Tool: CapCut Online (Easiest, Fastest)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "CapCut isn't working or is missing a feature"
- Switch to Clipchamp as backup
- Both tools have similar interfaces
- Check if browser needs updating
- Try incognito/private mode
- Clear browser cache and cookies

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Your Preferred Workflow: Simplified Loom → CapCut Online (+ Clipchamp if needed)

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

## Your Primary Tool: CapCut Online (Easiest, Fastest)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "CapCut isn't working or is missing a feature"
- Switch to Clipchamp as backup
- Both tools have similar interfaces
- Check if browser needs updating
- Try incognito/private mode
- Clear browser cache and cookies

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

---

1. ✅ Loom - Record screen demo with audio
2. ✅ Audacity - Record high-quality narration
3. ✅ CapCut Online - Primary video editor
4. ✅ Clipchamp - Backup/complement if needed

**Your Workflow:**
1. Record in Loom → Download MP4
2. Record narration in Audacity → Export WAV
3. Upload both to CapCut → Replace audio → Add AI captions
4. Export final video → Done!

**Estimated Time:** 3.5-5 hours total

**Why This Works:**
- Fastest path to professional results
- No software installation required (all online)
- AI features save time on captions
- Backup option ready if needed

## Troubleshooting

### "Loom video quality is poor"
- Use desktop app instead of browser
- Check internet connection during recording
- Record at smaller screen resolution
- Download highest quality version

### "Audio doesn't sync with video in editor"
- Both files must have same frame rate
- Try using timecode markers
- Cut video into sections and sync each part
- Re-export from Loom at different settings

### "CapCut isn't working or is missing a feature"
- Switch to Clipchamp as backup
- Both tools have similar interfaces
- Check if browser needs updating
- Try incognito/private mode
- Clear browser cache and cookies

### "Audacity narration has background noise"
- Re-record in quieter space
- Use Noise Reduction effect more aggressively
- Use Gate effect to cut quiet parts
- Consider using USB microphone instead of built-in

### "Exported video file is huge"
- Use H.264 codec (not uncompressed)
- Reduce bitrate (8-10 Mbps is fine for 1080p)
- Check resolution isn't higher than needed
- Use MP4 container format

## Notes & Tips

- **Prioritize audio quality** - viewers forgive bad video but not bad audio
- **Practice your script** before recording to sound natural
- **Keep it simple** - don't over-edit or add too many effects
- **Watch your video multiple times** before submitting
- **Get feedback** from someone else if possible
- **Start early** - rendering can take longer than expected
- **Save often** - auto-save in editors isn't always reliable
- **Keep raw files** - you might need to re-edit later

### Time-Saving Tips
- Don't aim for perfection in Loom recording - you're re-doing audio anyway
- Record narration in small chunks that match video sections
- Use keyboard shortcuts in your editor (learn basic ones)
- Pre-write your script word-for-word (already done!)
- Do a 1-minute test run of entire workflow before full production

**Your Workflow:**
1. Record in Loom → Download MP4
2. Record narration in Audacity → Export WAV
3. Upload both to CapCut → Replace audio → Add AI captions
4. Export final video → Done!

**Estimated Time:** 3.5-5 hours total

**Why This Works:**
- Fastest path to professional results
- No software installation required (all online)
- AI features save time on captions
- Backup option ready if needed

```markdown
# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: Loom + Audacity + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (video editor - audio replacement and AI features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (Free, AI-powered video editor)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - ⬜ Built-in royalty-free music library

---

## Your Workflow: Loom → Audacity → CapCut Online

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

## Your Primary Tool: CapCut Online (Easiest, Fastest)

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

```markdown
# 2025.10.19 - Video Production Plan: Loom + Audacity + CapCut Workflow

## Overview
This document outlines YOUR specific production workflow for creating the final demo video using:
- **Loom** (online, all-in-one recording)
- **Audacity** (separate narration recording)
- **CapCut Online** (video editor - audio replacement and AI features)

## Your Tool Setup

### ✅ Tools You Already Have
- **OBS Studio** - Advanced screen recording (backup option)
- **Audacity** - Audio recording and editing

### 🆕 Tools You Need to Set Up
1. **Loom** (Online web-based tool)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.loom.com/signup
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ Or optionally download desktop app for better performance
   - **Note**: Free plan has limits (25 videos, 5 min max), consider if you need Pro

2. **CapCut Online** (Free, AI-powered video editor)
   - ⬜ Create free account at https://www.capcut.com/
   - ⬜ No download required - works in browser
   - ⬜ AI-powered features (auto-captions, voice enhancement)
   - ⬜ Export up to 1080p free
   - ⬜ Built-in royalty-free music library

---

## Your Workflow: Loom → Audacity → CapCut Online

### Phase 1: Initial Recording in Loom (All-in-One)

#### Step 1.1: Set Up Loom Account
1. Visit https://www.loom.com/signup
2. Create free account or sign in with Google/Microsoft
3. Grant browser permissions for camera and microphone
4. *Optional*: Download desktop app for better performance and reliability

#### Step 1.2: Record Demo in Loom (With Audio)
1. Launch Loom (web or desktop app)
2. Click "Start Recording"
3. Choose recording mode:
   - **Screen only** (recommended for demos)
   - Screen + Camera (if you want face overlay)
   - Camera only (not relevant for this)
4. Select which screen/window to capture
5. **Keep microphone ON** - record with live narration
6. Click "Start Recording"
7. Perform your demo following FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md or FINAL-DEMO-CUE-CARDS.md
8. Click "Finish" when done

**Tips for Loom Recording:**
- Do a 30-second test recording first
- Speak clearly and at moderate pace
- Don't worry about perfect delivery - you'll re-record audio
- Focus on smooth screen actions and good visual pacing
- Use Loom's pause feature if you need to gather thoughts

#### Step 1.3: Basic Loom Editing (Optional)
If your recording is pretty good, use Loom's AI features:
1. **Trim** unwanted sections using timeline
2. **Remove filler words** - AI auto-detects "um", "uh", etc.
3. **Remove silence** - AI cuts long pauses
4. Review and approve AI edits

#### Step 1.4: Download Loom Video
1. Click "Share" button in Loom
2. Click "Download" icon
3. Select quality (choose highest available)
4. Save MP4 file to your project folder
5. **Filename suggestion**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`

---

### Phase 2: Record High-Quality Narration in Audacity

#### Step 2.1: Set Up Recording Environment
1. Find quiet space with minimal echo
2. Close windows, turn off fans/AC temporarily
3. Test microphone levels in Audacity
4. Use pop filter if available (or improvise with sock over mic)

#### Step 2.2: Record Narration in Audacity
1. Launch Audacity
2. Check input device is correct microphone
3. Set recording level (aim for -12dB to -6dB peak)
4. Click red Record button
5. Read from FINAL-DEMO-VIDEO-SCRIPT.md
   - Record in sections matching video segments
   - Pause between sections (you'll edit later)
   - Re-do any sections that don't sound good
6. Click Stop when finished

**Pro Tip**: Watch your Loom video on another screen while recording narration in Audacity. This helps you pace your narration to match the visuals.

#### Step 2.3: Edit Audio in Audacity
1. **Select all** (Ctrl+A)
2. **Effect** → **Normalize** → OK (balances volume)
3. **Effect** → **Noise Reduction**:
   - Select a quiet section first
   - Get Noise Profile
   - Select all again
   - Apply Noise Reduction (default settings)
4. **Delete long pauses** between sections
5. **Add fade in/out**:
   - Select first 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade In
   - Select last 0.5 seconds
   - Effect → Fade Out
6. Listen to entire track, make adjustments

#### Step 2.4: Export Audio
1. **File** → **Export** → **Export Audio**
2. Format: **WAV (Microsoft) signed 16-bit PCM** (best quality)
   - Or MP3 (320 kbps) if file size matters
3. **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`
4. Add metadata if desired
5. Save to project folder

---

### Phase 3: Combine Video + New Audio in CapCut Online

1. **Go to https://www.capcut.com/**
2. Sign up/in (free account)
3. Click **Start creating**

4. **Upload Assets**:
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Raw-Loom-Recording.mp4`
   - Upload `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav`

5. **Add Video to Timeline**:
   - Drag video to timeline
   - Video and its audio track appear

6. **Replace Audio**:
   - Click on the audio portion of the video track
   - Click **Detach** or right-click → Detach audio
   - Delete the detached original audio
   - Drag your `CollabCanvas-Demo-Narration.wav` to audio track

7. **Sync and Adjust**:
   - Drag audio to sync with video
   - Trim as needed
   - Use zoom slider for precision

8. **Add AI Features (Optional)**:
   - Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
   - Choose language and style
   - Review and edit auto-generated captions

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose 1080p resolution
   - Download MP4 file
   - **Filename**: `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Your Backup/Complement Tool: Clipchamp

Use Clipchamp if you need additional features or if CapCut has any limitations:

1. **Go to https://clipchamp.com/** or open from Windows 11 Start menu
2. Create free Microsoft account or sign in
3. **Create new video**

4. **Import media**:
   - Click **Import media**
   - Upload both video and audio files

5. **Drag to timeline**:
   - Drag Loom video to timeline
   - It splits into video and audio tracks

6. **Replace audio**:
   - Select original audio track
   - Press Delete
   - Drag new narration file to audio track

7. **Adjust timing**:
   - Align audio with video
   - Trim edges if needed

8. **Auto-captions** (if desired):
   - Click **Captions** in sidebar
   - Choose **Auto-captions**
   - Select language
   - Review and edit

9. **Export**:
   - Click **Export** (top right)
   - Choose **1080p** (free)
   - Save as `CollabCanvas-Demo-FINAL.mp4`

---

## Combining CapCut + Clipchamp (If Needed)

If you want features from both editors:

**Strategy 1: Sequential Editing**
1. Do main edit in CapCut (audio replacement, AI captions)
2. Export from CapCut
3. Import to Clipchamp for additional polish (title cards, transitions)
4. Final export from Clipchamp

**Strategy 2: Feature-Based Choice**
- **Use CapCut for**: AI captions, voice effects, trendy transitions
- **Use Clipchamp for**: Brand kit, OneDrive integration, text overlays

**Recommended**: Start with CapCut only. Add Clipchamp only if you discover you need something specific it offers.

---

## Phase 4: Optional Enhancement

### Add Subtitles (Highly Recommended)
Both CapCut and Clipchamp support auto-captions:

**In CapCut:**
1. Click **Captions** → **Auto captions**
2. Choose language (English)
3. Review and edit auto-generated captions
4. Adjust timing if needed
5. Style subtitles (font, color, position)

**In Clipchamp:**
1. Click **Captions** in sidebar
2. Choose **Auto-captions**
3. Select language
4. Review and edit for accuracy (especially technical terms like "CollabCanvas")
5. Adjust style and timing

### Add Simple Graphics (Optional)
- Title card at beginning (3-5 seconds): "CollabCanvas Demo"
- "Thank you" or call-to-action at end
- Your name/contact info
- Section dividers between major features

### Background Music (Optional, Use Carefully)
- Only if it enhances the video
- Keep volume very low (10% max)
- Use royalty-free music:
  - YouTube Audio Library
  - Incompetech.com
  - Free Music Archive
- Fade out when speaking
- **Tip**: CapCut has built-in royalty-free music library

---
